F\dashv U\colon[0,1]\text{-}{\bf CDL}\longrightarrow{\bf Set}
.

17.
Real-enriched domains

For each real-enriched category
X
, let

\mathcal{J}X=\{\phi\in\mathcal{I}X\mid\phi~\text{has a colimit}\}.

Then, every forward Cauchy net of
X
has a Yoneda limit if and only if
\mathcal{J}X=\mathcal{I}X
.

The Yoneda embedding
{\sf y}\colon X\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}X
factors through
\mathcal{J}X
, so we’ll write
{\sf y}\colon X\longrightarrow\mathcal{J}X
for the Yoneda embedding with codomain restricted to
\mathcal{J}X
. It is clear that
{\sf y}\colon X\longrightarrow\mathcal{J}X
has a left adjoint
\colim\colon\mathcal{J}X\longrightarrow X
that sends each
\phi\in\mathcal{J}X
to its colimit.

Definition 17.1
.

(Waszkiewicz
[
84
]
) Suppose
X
is a real-enriched category. If
\colim\colon\mathcal{J}X\longrightarrow X
has a left adjoint, then we say that
X
is continuous. In other words,
X
is continuous if there exists a string of adjunctions

\wayb\dashv\colim\dashv{\sf y}\colon X\longrightarrow\mathcal{J}X.

A real-enriched domain is a Yoneda complete and continuous real-enriched category.

A real-enriched category
A
is a real-enriched domain if and only if it is a continuous
\mathbb{I}
-algebra, where
\mathbb{I}=(\mathcal{I},{\sf m},{\sf y})
is the ideal monad in
[0,1]\text{-}{\bf Cat}
; that means,
A
is a separated real-enriched category such that there is a string of adjunctions:

\wayb\dashv\colim\dashv{\sf y}\colon A\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}A.

For each ordered set
(X,\leq)
, it is clear that
\omega(X,\leq)
is a real-enriched domain if and only if
(X,\leq)
is a domain. But, the underlying ordered set of a real-enriched domain may fail to be a domain.

Example 17.2
.

(Yu and Zhang
[
88
]
)
In this example we assume that the implication operator
\rightarrow\colon[0,1]^{2}\longrightarrow[0,1]
is continuous at every point off the diagonal. Consider the real-enriched category
X
, where
X=[0,1]\cup\{\infty\}
and

X(x,y)=\begin{cases}x\rightarrow y&x,y\in[0,1],\\
1&x=y=\infty,\\
0&x\in[0,1],y=\infty,\\
y&x=\infty,y\in[0,1].\end{cases}

Since forward Cauchy nets in
X
are forward Cauchy nets in
([0,1],\alpha_{L})
plus constant nets with value
\infty
,
X
is Yoneda complete. Since
\rightarrow\colon[0,1]^{2}\longrightarrow[0,1]
is continuous at every point off the diagonal, a routine calculation shows that

{\sf d}\colon X\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}X,\quad{\sf d}(x)=\begin{cases}X(-,\infty)&x=\infty,\\
X(-,0)&x=0,\\
\sup\limits_{y<x}X(-,y)&x\in(0,1]\end{cases}

is left adjoint to
\colim\colon\mathcal{I}X\longrightarrow X
, so
X
is a real-enriched domain. But
X_{0}
, as visualized below, is not a domain.
0
1
\infty

Let
X
be a real-enriched category. The
way below distributor
of
X
refers to the distributor
\mathfrak{w}\colon X{\longrightarrow\hskip-13.3472pt{\circ}\hskip 7.3194pt}X
given by

\mathfrak{w}(y,x)=\inf_{\phi\in\mathcal{J}X}(X(x,\colim\phi)\rightarrow\phi(y)).

It is clear that
\mathfrak{w}(y,x)\leq X(y,x)
.

Proposition 17.3
.

(Waszkiewicz
[
84
]
)
A real-enriched category
X
is continuous if and only if for all
x\in X
, the weight
\mathfrak{w}(-,x)
belongs to
\mathcal{J}X
and has
x
as a colimit. In this case,
\wayb x=\mathfrak{w}(-,x)
.

Proof.

We prove the necessity first. Suppose
X
is continuous. We show that for each
x\in X
,
\mathfrak{w}(-,x)=\wayb x
, hence
\mathfrak{w}(-,x)
belongs to
\mathcal{J}X
and has
x
as colimit. Since
\colim\wayb x=x
, then

\mathfrak{w}(-,x)\leq X(x,\colim\wayb x)\rightarrow\wayb x=\wayb x.

On the other hand, since for all
\phi\in\mathcal{J}X
, we have

X(x,\colim\phi)\with\wayb x=\mathcal{J}X(\wayb x,\phi)\with\wayb x\leq\phi,

then
\wayb x\leq X(x,\colim\phi)\rightarrow\phi
for all
\phi\in\mathcal{J}X
, hence
\wayb x\leq\mathfrak{w}(-,x)
.

For sufficiency we show that the assignment
x\mapsto\wayb x\coloneqq\mathfrak{w}(-,x)
defines a left adjoint of
\colim\colon\mathcal{J}X\longrightarrow X
. Let
x\in X
and
\phi\in\mathcal{J}X
. By definition we have
\wayb x\leq X(x,\colim\phi)\rightarrow\phi
, hence

X(x,\colim\phi)\leq\mathcal{J}X(\wayb x,\phi).

Since taking colimit is functorial
\mathcal{J}X\longrightarrow X
and
\colim\wayb x=x
, then

\mathcal{J}X(\wayb x,\phi)\leq X(\colim\wayb x,\colim\phi)=X(x,\colim\phi).

Therefore,
\wayb
is a left adjoint of
\colim\colon\mathcal{J}X\longrightarrow X
, as desired. ∎

The following conclusion, contained in Waszkiewicz
[
84
, Section 3]
, extends the fact that the way below relation of a continuous partially ordered set is interpolative (see Corollary
2.32
).

Proposition 17.4
.

The way-below distributor
\mathfrak{w}
of a continuous real-enriched category interpolates in the sense that
\mathfrak{w}\circ\mathfrak{w}=\mathfrak{w}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
17.3
,
\mathfrak{w}(-,x)
is the least element of
\mathcal{J}X
having
x
as colimit. Since
\mathfrak{w}(y,x)\leq X(y,x)
for all
y,x\in X
, it follows that
\mathfrak{w}\circ\mathfrak{w}(-,x)\leq\mathfrak{w}(-,x)
, so we only need to show that
\mathfrak{w}\circ\mathfrak{w}(-,x)
belongs to
\mathcal{I}X
and has
x
as colimit.

Step 1
.
\mathfrak{w}\circ\mathfrak{w}(-,x)\in\mathcal{I}X
.

Let
\lambda,\mu\in\mathcal{P}X
. Since

\sub_{X}(\mathfrak{w}(-,y),\lambda)\with X(z,y)\with\mathfrak{w}(-,z)\leq\lambda,

it follows that

\sub_{X}(\mathfrak{w}(-,y),\lambda)\with X(z,y)\leq\sub_{X}(\mathfrak{w}(-,z),\lambda),

so the assignment
y\mapsto\sub_{X}(\mathfrak{w}(-,y),\lambda)
defines a weight of
X
. Then,

\displaystyle\quad\quad~\sub_{X}(\mathfrak{w}\circ\mathfrak{w}(-,x),\lambda\vee\mu)

\displaystyle=\inf_{y\in X}\sub_{X}(\mathfrak{w}(y,x)\with\mathfrak{w}(-,y),\lambda\vee\mu)

\displaystyle=\inf_{y\in X}\Big(\mathfrak{w}(y,x)\rightarrow\sub_{X}(\mathfrak{w}(-,y),\lambda\vee\mu)\Big)

\displaystyle=\inf_{y\in X}\Big(\mathfrak{w}(y,x)\rightarrow(\sub_{X}(\mathfrak{w}(-,y),\lambda)\vee\sub_{X}(\mathfrak{w}(-,y),\mu)\Big)

\displaystyle=\inf_{y\in X}\Big(\mathfrak{w}(y,x)\rightarrow\sub_{X}(\mathfrak{w}(-,y),\lambda)\Big)\vee\inf_{y\in X}\Big(\mathfrak{w}(y,x)\rightarrow\sub_{X}(\mathfrak{w}(-,y),\mu)\Big)

\displaystyle=\sub_{X}(\mathfrak{w}\circ\mathfrak{w}(-,x),\lambda)\vee\sub_{X}(\mathfrak{w}\circ\mathfrak{w}(-,x),\mu).

Likewise, one verifies that for all
\lambda\in\mathcal{P}X
and
r\in[0,1]
,

\sub_{X}(\mathfrak{w}\circ\mathfrak{w}(-,x),r\with\lambda)=r\with\sub_{X}(\mathfrak{w}\circ\mathfrak{w}(-,x),\lambda).

This proves that
\mathfrak{w}\circ\mathfrak{w}(-,x)\in\mathcal{I}X
.

Step 2
.
\colim\mathfrak{w}\circ\mathfrak{w}(-,x)=x
.

For all
b\in X
,

\displaystyle X(x,b)

\displaystyle=\inf_{y\in X}(\mathfrak{w}(y,x)\rightarrow X(y,b))

\displaystyle(\colim\mathfrak{w}(-,x)=x)

\displaystyle=\inf_{y\in X}(\mathfrak{w}(y,x)\rightarrow\mathcal{P}X(\mathfrak{w}(-,y),X(-,b)))

\displaystyle(\colim\mathfrak{w}(-,y)=y)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}X(\mathfrak{w}\circ\mathfrak{w}(-,x),X(-,b)),

hence
\colim\mathfrak{w}\circ\mathfrak{w}(-,x)=x
.
∎

Corollary 17.5
.

Let
X
be a continuous real-enriched category. Then for each
x\in X
, the functor

\mathfrak{w}(x,-)\colon X\longrightarrow([0,1],\alpha_{L})

is Yoneda continuous.

Proof.

We need to show that
\mathfrak{w}(x,\colim\phi)=\colim_{\phi}\mathfrak{w}(x,-)
for each ideal
\phi
of
X
that has a colimit. Since
\mathfrak{w}(x,-)
is a functor, it suffices to check that
\mathfrak{w}(x,\colim\phi)\leq\colim_{\phi}\mathfrak{w}(x,-)
. This is easy since

\displaystyle{\colim}_{\phi}\mathfrak{w}(x,-)

\displaystyle=\sup_{y\in X}\phi(y)\with\mathfrak{w}(x,y)

\displaystyle\geq\sup_{y\in X}\mathfrak{w}(y,\colim\phi)\with\mathfrak{w}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{w}(x,\colim\phi).\qed

Definition 17.6
.

An element
a
of a real-enriched category
X
is compact if the functor

X(a,-)\colon X\longrightarrow{\sf V}

is Yoneda continuous, where
{\sf V}=([0,1],\alpha_{L})
.

This is a direct extension of compact elements (also called finite elements) in generalized metric spaces, see e.g. Bonsangue, van Breugel and Rutten
[
9
]
, and Goubaul-Larrecq
[
23
]
.

It follows from Proposition
8.2
that a Cauchy weight of a real-enriched category
X
is precisely an ideal of
X
that is a compact element of
\mathcal{P}X
.

Lemma 17.7
.

For each real-enriched category
X
and each element
a
of
X
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

a
is compact.

(2)

X(a,\colim\phi)=\phi(a)
for all
\phi\in\mathcal{J}X
.

(3)

\mathfrak{w}(-,a)=X(-,a)
.

Proof.

For all
\phi\in\mathcal{J}X
, by Example
6.1
the colimit of
X(a,-)\colon X\longrightarrow{\sf V}
weighted by
\phi
is equal to the composite of
X(a,-)
and
\phi
; that is,
\colim_{\phi}X(a,-)=\phi\circ X(a,-)=\phi(a)
. This proves
(1)\Leftrightarrow(2)
.

(2)\Rightarrow(3)
For each
y\in X
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{w}(y,a)

\displaystyle=\inf_{\phi\in\mathcal{J}X}(X(a,\colim\phi)\rightarrow\phi(y))

\displaystyle=\inf_{\phi\in\mathcal{J}X}(\phi(a)\rightarrow\phi(y))

\displaystyle=X(y,a).

(3)\Rightarrow(2)
Since taking colimit is functorial, then

\phi(a)=\mathcal{P}X(X(-,a),\phi)\leq X(a,\colim\phi)

for all
\phi\in\mathcal{J}X
. On the other hand, since by definition

\mathfrak{w}(y,a)=\inf_{\phi\in\mathcal{J}X}(X(a,\colim\phi)\rightarrow\phi(y)),

then
X(a,\colim\phi)\leq\mathfrak{w}(y,a)\rightarrow\phi(y)
for all
y\in X
and all
\phi\in\mathcal{J}X
, hence

X(a,\colim\phi)\leq\mathcal{P}X(\mathfrak{w}(-,a),\phi)=\mathcal{P}X(X(-,a),\phi)=\phi(a).

Therefore,
X(a,\colim\phi)=\phi(a)
. ∎

A real-enriched category
X
is
algebraic
if each of its elements is a Yoneda limit of a forward Cauchy net consisting of compact elements. Said differently,
X
is algebraic if for each
x\in X
, there is an ideal
\phi
of the real-enriched category
K(X)
composed of compact elements of
X
such that
x
is a colimit of the inclusion functor
i\colon K(X)\longrightarrow X
weighted by
\phi
.

Example 17.8
.

The real-enriched category
{\sf V}=([0,1],\alpha_{L})
is algebraic if and only if so is its opposite
{\sf V}^{\rm op}
, if and only if the continuous t-norm
\with
is Archimedean. We distinguish three cases.

Case 1.
\&
is isomorphic to the Łukasiewicz t-norm. Then all elements of
{\sf V}
and
{\sf V}^{\rm op}
are compact, so both
{\sf V}
and
{\sf V}^{\rm op}
are algebraic.

Case 2.
\&
is isomorphic to the product t-norm. Then all elements of
{\sf V}
are compact and
0
is the only non-compact element of
{\sf V}^{\rm op}
, so, both
{\sf V}
and
{\sf V}^{\rm op}
are algebraic.

Case 3.
\&
is not Archimedean. Let
b
be an idempotent element other than
0
and
1
. For each
a\in[0,b]
consider the ideal
\phi=\sup_{x<a}{\sf V}(-,x)
of
{\sf V}
. Since
\colim\phi=a
and

\phi(a)=\sup_{x<a}(a\rightarrow x)\leq b<1={\sf V}(a,\colim\phi),

it follows that
a
is not compact. By help of this fact one readily verifies that
{\sf V}
is not algebraic. Likewise, one sees that each
a\in[0,b]
is not compact in
{\sf V}^{\rm op}
, so
{\sf V}^{\rm op}
is not algebraic either.

Proposition 17.9
.

Every algebraic real-enriched category is continuous.

Proof.

We need to check that the functor
\colim\colon\mathcal{J}X\longrightarrow X
has a left adjoint. For each
a\in X
, pick a forward Cauchy net
\{a_{i}\}_{i\in D}
of compact elements of
X
with
a
as a Yoneda limit and let

\wayb a=\inf_{i\in D}\sup_{j\geq i}X(-,a_{j}).

Then
\wayb a
is an ideal of
X
with
a
as a colimit (Lemma
9.11
), hence it is an element of
\mathcal{J}X
. Since
\wayb a
is a Yoneda limit of the forward Cauchy net
\{X(-,a_{i})\}_{i\in D}
in
\mathcal{P}X
(Lemma
9.7
), it is then a Yoneda limit of
\{X(-,a_{i})\}_{i\in D}
in
\mathcal{J}X
. Thus, for every
\phi\in\mathcal{J}X
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}X(\wayb a,\phi)

\displaystyle=\inf_{i\in D}\sup_{j\geq i}\mathcal{J}X(X(-,a_{j}),\phi)

\displaystyle(\text{$\wayb a$ is a Yoneda limit})

\displaystyle=\inf_{i\in D}\sup_{j\geq i}\phi(a_{j})

\displaystyle({\rm Yoneda~lemma})

\displaystyle=\inf_{i\in D}\sup_{j\geq i}X(a_{j},\colim\phi)

\displaystyle(a_{j}{\rm~is~compact})

\displaystyle=X(a,\colim\phi),

\displaystyle(a~\text{is a Yoneda limit})

which shows
\wayb
is a left adjoint of
\colim
. ∎

Proposition 17.10
.

A real-enriched category is a real-enriched domain if and only if it is a retract of an algebraic real-enriched category in the category of
\mathbb{I}
-algebras.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition
15.6
and the fact that for each real-enriched category
X
, the category
\mathcal{I}X
of ideals of
X
is algebraic. ∎

Proposition 17.11
.

For a Smyth completable real-enriched category
X
, the functor
{\sf y}\colon X\longrightarrow\mathcal{J}X
is both left and right adjoint to
\colim\colon\mathcal{J}X\longrightarrow X
. In particular,
X
is continuous.

Proof.

First we show that every element of
X
is compact; that is,
X(a,\colim\phi)=\phi(a)
for all
a\in X
and all
\phi\in\mathcal{J}X
. Since
X
is Smyth completable,
\phi
is Cauchy, hence representable because it has a colimit. This means
\phi=X(-,b)
for some
b\in X
. Therefore,

\displaystyle X(a,\colim\phi)

\displaystyle=X(\colim X(-,a),b)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}X(X(-,a),X(-,b))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}X(X(-,a),\phi)

\displaystyle=\phi(a).

Next we show that
{\sf y}\colon X\longrightarrow\mathcal{J}X
is left adjoint to
\colim\colon\mathcal{J}X\longrightarrow X
. This is easy, since
\mathcal{J}X({\sf y}(a),\phi)=\phi(a)=X(a,\colim\phi)
for all
a\in X
and
\phi\in\mathcal{J}X
. ∎

Corollary 17.12
.

Every Smyth completeable real-enriched category is algebraic, hence continuous.

The equivalence between (1) and (3) in the next corollary is due to Ali-Akbari, Honarii, Pourmahdian and Rezaii
[
2
]
.

Corollary 17.13
.

For each separated real-enriched category
X
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

X
is Smyth complete.

(2)

The functor
{\sf y}\colon X\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathcal{I}X
is both left and right adjoint to the functor
\colim\colon\mathcal{I}X\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>X
.

(3)

Every forward Cauchy net of
X
has a Yoneda limit and every element of
X
is compact.

Theorem
9.18
shows that when the continuous t-norm
\&
is Archimedean, Yoneda complete real-enriched categories can be characterized purely in terms of their set of formal balls. Theorem
17.16
below shows that in this case, under a mild assumption, there is a parallel result for continuous real-enriched categories.

The following notion is a direct extension of that of
standard quasi-metric space
in Goubault-Larrecq and Ng
[
24
, Definition 2.1]
.

Definition 17.14
.

Suppose
\&
is a continuous Archimedean t-norm. A real-enriched category
X
is standard provided that for each directed subset
\{(x_{i},r_{i})\}_{i\in D}
of
\mathrm{B}X
with
\sup_{i\in D}r_{i}>0
, if
\{(x_{i},r_{i})\}_{i\in D}
has a join, then so do the following subsets:

(i)

\{(x_{i},t\with r_{i})\}_{i\in D}
for each
t>0
; and

(ii)

\{(x_{i},t\rightarrow r_{i})\}_{i\in D}
for
t=\sup_{i\in D}r_{i}
.

It follows from Lemma
9.20
and Lemma
9.19
that, when
\&
is a continuous Archimedean t-norm, every Yoneda complete real-enriched category is standard. The real-enriched category in Example
9.25
is not standard, otherwise, it would be Yoneda complete by Lemma
9.24
.

Lemma 17.15
.

Suppose
\&
is a continuous Archimedean t-norm; suppose
X
is a standard real-enriched category and
\{(x_{i},r_{i})\}_{i\in D}
is a directed subset of
\mathrm{B}X
with
\sup_{i\in D}r_{i}>0
. If
(x,r)
is a join of
\{(x_{i},r_{i})\}_{i\in D}
, then

(i)

x
is a Yoneda limit of the forward Cauchy net
\{x_{i}\}_{i\in D}
and
r=\sup_{i\in D}r_{i}
;

(ii)

(x,1)
is a join of
\{(x_{i},r\rightarrow r_{i})\}_{i\in D}
;

(iii)

for each
t\in[0,1]
,
(x,t\with r)
is a join of
\{(x_{i},t\with r_{i})\}_{i\in D}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that
x
is a Yoneda limit of the forward Cauchy net
\{x_{i}\}_{i\in D}
, the other conclusions will follow by Lemma
9.19
.
Let
t=\sup_{i\in D}r_{i}
. Since
\&
is Archimedean, then
\sup_{i\in D}(t\rightarrow r_{i})=1
. Since
X
is standard, the directed set
\{(x_{i},t\rightarrow r_{i})\}_{i\in D}
has a join, say
(b,1)
. By Lemma
9.24
,
b
is a Yoneda limit of the forward Cauchy net
\{x_{i}\}_{i\in D}
, then
(b,t)
is a join of
\{(x_{i},r_{i})\}_{i\in D}
by Lemma
9.19
. Therefore,
r=t
and
x
is isomorphic to
b
, so
x
is a Yoneda limit of
\{x_{i}\}_{i\in D}
. ∎

The following theorem is a combination and a slight improvement of related results in Goubault-Larrecq and Ng
[
24
, Section 3]
and Kostanek and Waszkiewicz
[
43
, Section 9]
. It implies in particular that for standard quasi-metric spaces, continuity in the sense of Definition
17.1
coincides with that in the sense of Goubault-Larrecq and Ng
[
24
, Definition 3.10]
.

Theorem 17.16
.

Suppose
\&
is a continuous Archimedean t-norm and
X
is a standard real-enriched category. The following are equivalent:

(1)

X
is continuous.

(2)

The ordered set
\mathrm{B}X
is continuous.

In this case, the way below distributor
\mathfrak{w}
of
X
and the way below relation
\ll
of
\mathrm{B}X
determine each other via

(x,r)\ll(y,s)\iff r<s\with\mathfrak{w}(x,y)

for all
x,y\in X
and
r,s>0
.

Proof.

(1)\Rightarrow(2)
First we present a fact that will be used in Step 1 below.
Suppose
X
is a real-enriched category and
\{(x_{i},r_{i})\}_{i\in D}
is a directed subset of
\mathrm{B}X
with
\sup_{i\in D}r_{i}=1
.
Let
\phi_{D}=\sup_{i\in D}\inf_{j\geq i}X(-,x_{j})
and let
\mathrm{B}\phi_{D}=\{(x,r)\mid\phi_{D}(x)>r\}
. By Proposition
9.13
,
\mathrm{B}\phi_{D}
is a directed subset of
\mathrm{B}X
. We assert that for each
(x,r)\in\mathrm{B}\phi_{D}
, there exists
j\in D
such that
(x,r)\sqsubseteq(x_{j},r_{j})
. To see this, suppose
(x,r)\in\mathrm{B}\phi_{D}
. Since
\phi_{D}(x)=\sup_{i\in D}\inf_{j\geq i}X(x,x_{j})
, there is
i\in D
such that
r<\inf_{j\geq i}X(x,x_{j})
. Since
\sup_{j\geq i}r_{j}=1
, there is
j\geq i
such that
r<r_{j}\with X(x,x_{j})
, so
(x,r)\sqsubseteq(x_{j},r_{j})
.

Now we turn to the proof of the conclusion. Since
X
is continuous, the functor
\colim\colon\mathcal{J}X\longrightarrow X
has a left adjoint
\wayb\colon X\longrightarrow\mathcal{J}X
. For each
y\in X
, let

d(y)=\{(x,r)\in\mathrm{B}X\mid r<\mathfrak{w}(x,y)\}.

Then
d(y)
is a directed subset of
\mathrm{B}X
with
(y,1)
as a join. In the following we prove in two steps that every element of
\mathrm{B}X
is the join of the elements way below it, so
\mathrm{B}X
is continuous.

Step 1
. We show that
(x,r)\ll(y,1)
for all
(x,r)\in d(y)
, hence
(y,1)
is the join of the elements way below it.

Let
(x,r)\in d(y)
. Suppose
D=\{(z_{i},t_{i})\}_{i\in D}
is a directed subset of
\mathrm{B}X
with a join, say
(z,t)
, larger than
(y,1)
. It is clear that
t=1
and
X(y,z)=1
. Since
X
is standard,
\sup_{i\in D}t_{i}=1
and
z
is a Yoneda limit of the forward Cauchy net
\{z_{i}\}_{i\in D}
. So,
z
is a colimit of the ideal
\phi_{D}
generated by
\{z_{i}\}_{i\in D}
. Since
\wayb
is left adjoint to
\colim
we have

\mathcal{J}X(\wayb z,\phi_{D})=X(z,\colim\phi_{D})=X(z,z)=1,

then
\wayb z\leq\phi_{D}
and
d(z)\subseteq\mathrm{B}\phi_{D}
. Since
X(y,z)=1
and
\wayb
is a functor, then
\wayb y\leq\wayb z
. Therefore,
d(y)\subseteq d(z)\subseteq\mathrm{B}\phi_{D}
and consequently,
(x,r)\sqsubseteq(z_{j},t_{j})
for some
j\in D
. This proves that
(x,r)\ll(y,1)
.

Step 2
. We show that
(x,r)\ll(y,s)
whenever
r<s\with\mathfrak{w}(x,y)
, in particular
(x,s\with r)\ll(y,s)
for all
(x,r)\in d(y)
, hence by Step 1,
(y,s)
is the join of the elements way below it.

Suppose
D=\{(z_{i},t_{i})\}_{i\in D}
is a directed subset of
\mathrm{B}X
with a join, say
(z,t)
, larger than
(y,s)
. We wish to show that
(x,r)\sqsubseteq(z_{j},t_{j})
for some
j\in D
. Since
X
is standard, the monotone net
\{t_{i}\}_{i\in D}
converges to
t
. Since
s\leq t\with X(y,z)
and
r<s\with\mathfrak{w}(x,y)
, it follows that

r<t\with X(y,z)\with\mathfrak{w}(x,y)\leq t\with\mathfrak{w}(x,z).

Let
\phi=\sup_{i\in D}\inf_{j\geq i}X(-,z_{j})
. Since
\phi
is an ideal of
X
with
z
as a colimit, it follows that
\mathfrak{w}(-,z)\leq\phi
, then

\displaystyle r

\displaystyle<t\with\mathfrak{w}(x,z)

\displaystyle=t\with\sup_{w\in X}(\mathfrak{w}(w,z)\with\mathfrak{w}(x,w))

\displaystyle\leq t\with\sup_{w\in X}(\phi(w)\with\mathfrak{w}(x,w))

\displaystyle=t\with\sup_{w\in X}\Big[\Big(\sup_{i\in D}\inf_{j\geq i}X(w,z_{j})\Big)\with\mathfrak{w}(x,w)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq t\with\sup_{i\in D}\Big(\sup_{w\in X}\inf_{j\geq i}X(w,z_{j})\with\mathfrak{w}(x,w)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\sup_{i\in D}t_{i}\Big)\with\Big(\sup_{i\in D}\inf_{j\geq i}\mathfrak{w}(x,z_{j})\Big).

Therefore, there is some
j\in D
such that

r<t_{j}\with\mathfrak{w}(x,z_{j})\leq t_{j}\with X(x,z_{j}),

hence
(x,r)\sqsubseteq(z_{j},t_{j})
.

(2)\Rightarrow(1)
First we present a fact that will be used in Step 3 below.
Suppose
X
is a real-enriched category,
\phi\in\mathcal{J}X
,
(x,r)\in\mathrm{B}X
. We assert that if
(x,r)\ll(\colim\phi,1)
, then
\phi(x)>r
. To see this, index the directed subset
\{(y,s)\mid\phi(y)>s\}
of
\mathrm{B}X
as
\{(y_{i},s_{i})\}_{i\in D}
. Then
\sup_{i\in D}s_{i}=1
and
\{y_{i}\}_{i\in D}
is a forward Cauchy net with
\colim\phi
as a Yoneda limit. By Lemma
9.19

(\colim\phi,1)
is a join of
\{(y_{i},s_{i})\}_{i\in D}
, then
(x,r)\sqsubseteq(y_{i},s_{i})
for some
i\in D
, hence

r\leq s_{i}\with X(x,y_{i})<\phi(y_{i})\with X(x,y_{i})\leq\phi(x),

showing that
\phi(x)>r
.

Now we turn to the proof of the conclusion.
For each
y\in X
, let

D=\{(x,r)\mid(x,r)\ll(y,1)\}.

Then
D
is a directed set of
\mathrm{B}X
with
(y,1)
as a join. Index
D
by itself, i.e.
D=\{(x_{i},r_{i})\}_{i\in D}
. Then
\sup_{i\in D}r_{i}=1
and
\{x_{i}\}_{i\in D}
is a forward Cauchy net. Let

k(y)=\sup_{i\in D}\inf_{j\geq i}X(-,x_{j}).

Then
k(y)
is an ideal of
X
with
y
as a colimit. We show that the assignment
y\mapsto k(y)
defines a left adjoint of
\colim\colon\mathcal{J}X\longrightarrow X
, so
X
is continuous. We do this in three steps.

Step 1
.
k(y)=\sup_{i\in D}r_{i}\with X(-,x_{i})
. This implies that, if
k(y)(x)>r
then
(x,r)\ll(y,1)
.

For any
(x_{i},r_{i})\sqsubseteq(x_{j},r_{j})
we have
r_{i}\leq X(x_{i},x_{j})
, then
r_{i}\with X(-,x_{i})\leq X(-,x_{j})
. Therefore,

r_{i}\with X(-,x_{i})\leq\inf_{j\geq i}X(-,x_{j}),

hence

k(y)\geq\sup_{i\in D}r_{i}\with X(-,x_{i}).

To see the converse inequality, let
t<1
. Since
r_{i}
tends to
1
, there exists
i_{0}\in D
such that
r_{j}\geq t
whenever
j\geq i_{0}
. Then

t\with k(y)\leq\sup_{i\geq i_{0}}\inf_{j\geq i}r_{j}\with X(-,x_{j})\leq\sup_{i\in D}r_{i}\with X(-,x_{i}),

hence
k(y)\leq\sup_{i\in D}r_{i}\with X(-,x_{i})
by arbitrariness of
t
.

Step 2
. If
(x,r)\ll(y,1)
, then
(x,t\with r)\ll(y,t)
for all
t\in[0,1]
.

If
t\with r=0
there is nothing to prove, so in the following we assume that
t\with r>0
. Index the directed set
\{(z,s)\mid(z,s)\ll(y,t)\}
as
\{(z_{i},s_{i})\}_{i\in D}
. Since
\mathrm{B}X
is continuous,
(y,t)
is a join of
\{(z_{i},s_{i})\}_{i\in D}
, then
(y,1)
is a join of the directed set
\{(z_{i},t\rightarrow s_{i})\}_{i\in D}
, so there exists
i\in D
such that
(x,r)\leq(z_{i},t\rightarrow s_{i})
, hence

(x,t\with r)\leq(z_{i},s_{i})\ll(y,t).

Step 3
.
k\colon X\longrightarrow\mathcal{J}X
is left adjoint to
\colim\colon\mathcal{J}X\longrightarrow X
.

First we check that
k
is a functor. Suppose
y_{1},y_{2}\in X
and
t=X(y_{1},y_{2})
. If
(x,r)\ll(y_{1},1)
, then
(x,t\with r)\ll(y_{1},t)
by Step 2. Since
(y_{1},t)\sqsubseteq(y_{2},1)
, it follows that
(x,t\with r)\ll(y_{2},1)
, then

\displaystyle t\with k(y_{1})

\displaystyle=\sup\{t\with r\with X(-,x)\mid(x,r)\ll(y_{1},1)\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup\{r\with X(-,x)\mid(x,r)\ll(y_{2},1)\}

\displaystyle\leq k(y_{2}),

so
k
is a functor. By Theorem
4.5

it remains to check that for all
y\in X
and
\phi\in\mathcal{J}X
it holds that

k(y)\leq\phi\iff y\sqsubseteq\colim\phi.

The direction
\implies
is obvious since
\colim
is functorial and
\colim k(y)=y
. For the other direction it suffices to check that
k(\colim\phi)\leq\phi
. This follows from that
\phi(x)>r
whenever
(x,r)\ll(\colim\phi,1)
. ∎

Corollary 17.17
.

(Goubault-Larrecq and Ng
[
24
, Theorem 3.7]
)
Suppose
\&
is a continuous Archimedean t-norm. Then a Yoneda complete real-enriched category
X
is a real-enriched domain if and only if
\mathrm{B}X
is directed complete and continuous.

The following corollary extends the characterization of Smyth complete quasi-metric spaces in Romaguera and Valero
[
70
, Theorem 3.1]
.

Corollary 17.18
.

Suppose
\&
is a continuous Archimedean t-norm. Then for each separated and standard real-enriched category
X
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

X
is Smyth complete.

(2)

\mathrm{B}X
is directed complete, continuous, and

(x,r)\ll(y,s)\iff r<s\with X(x,y)

for all
x,y\in X
and
r,s>0
.

(3)

\mathrm{B}X
is directed complete, continuous, and

(x,r)\ll(y,1)\iff r<X(x,y)

for all
x,y\in X
and
r>0
.

Proof.

(1)\Rightarrow(2)
Since
X
is Smyth comple, it is a real-enriched domain with way below distributor given by
\mathfrak{w}(x,y)=X(x,y)
. Then, from Theorem
9.18
and Theorem
17.16
it follows that
\mathrm{B}X
is directed complete and continuous, and that for all
x,y\in X
and
r,s>0
,

(x,r)\ll(y,s)\iff r<s\with X(x,y).

(2)\Rightarrow(3)
Trivial.

(3)\Rightarrow(1)
By Theorem
9.18
and Theorem
17.16
,
X
is a real-enriched domain with way below distributor given by
\mathfrak{w}(x,y)=X(x,y)
, then every element of
X
is compact and
X
is Smyth complete. ∎

Consider the real-enriched category
X
in Example
9.26
. Since every forward Cauchy net of
X
is eventually constant, it follows that
X
is Smyth complete. But, the ordered set of formal balls of
X
fails to be directed complete. This shows that in corollaries
17.17
and
17.18
the assumption that
\&
is Archimedean is indispensable.

Real-enriched continuous lattices

A complete (hence cocomplete) real-enriched domain is called a
real-enriched continuous lattice
.

Before proceeding with our investigation of real-enriched continuous lattices, we present a property of ideals of separated and complete real-enriched categories.

Let
X
be a real-enriched category. For each ideal
I
of the ordered set
X_{0}
, the conical weight

\Lambda(I)\coloneqq\sup_{x\in I}X(-,x)

is clearly an ideal of
X
. Conversely, if
X
is finitely complete and finitely cocomplete, with help of Proposition
7.5
one verifies that for each ideal
\phi
of
X
, the set

\chi(\phi)\coloneqq\{x\in X\mid\phi(x)=1\}

is an ideal of the ordered set
X_{0}
.

Lemma 17.19
.

If
X
is a finitely complete and finitely cocomplete real-enriched category and
\idl X_{0}
is the set of ideals of the ordered set
X_{0}
, then
\Lambda\colon\idl X_{0}\longrightarrow(\mathcal{I}X)_{0}
is left adjoint to
\chi\colon(\mathcal{I}X)_{0}\longrightarrow\idl X_{0}
.

Proof.

Suppose
I
is an ideal of the ordered set
X_{0}
and

\phi
is an ideal of
X
. Then

I\subseteq\chi(\phi)\iff\forall x\in I,\phi(x)=1\iff\Lambda(I)\leq\phi,

so
\Lambda\dashv\chi
. ∎

Proposition 17.20
.

Let
X
be a separated and complete real-enriched category. Then for each weight
\phi
of
X
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

\phi
is an ideal of
X
.

(2)

\phi=\Lambda(I)
for some ideal
I
of the complete lattice
X_{0}
.

In this case, the colimit of
\phi
is a join of
\chi(\phi)
in the complete lattice
X_{0}
.

Proof.

(1)\Rightarrow(2)
We show that the ideal
\chi(\phi)
satisfies the requirement; that is,
\Lambda\circ\chi(\phi)=\phi
. Since
\Lambda
is left adjoint to
\chi
, then
\Lambda\circ\chi(\phi)\leq\phi
. For the converse inequality, pick a forward Cauchy net
\{x_{i}\}_{i\in D}
of
X
such that

\phi(x)=\sup_{i\in D}\inf_{j\geq i}X(x,x_{j}).

Let

I=\Big\{x\in X\mid x\sqsubseteq\inf_{j\geq i}x_{j}~\text{for some}~i\in D\Big\},

where
\inf_{j\geq i}x_{j}
denotes the meet in
X_{0}
.
Then,
I
is an ideal of the ordered set
X_{0}
and

\phi=\sup_{x\in I}X(-,x),

so
I\subseteq\chi(\phi)
and consequently,
\phi\leq\Lambda\circ\chi(\phi)
.

(2)\Rightarrow(1)
Trivial, since every ideal of the ordered set
X_{0}
may be viewed as a forward Cauchy net of
X
.

Finally, we check that the colimit of
\phi
is the join
\sup\chi(\phi)
of
\chi(\phi)
in the complete lattice
X_{0}
. Let
I
be the ideal of
X_{0}
given in
(1)\Rightarrow(2)
. Since
I
is contained in
\chi(\phi)
and
\colim\phi=\sup_{z\in X}\phi(z)\otimes z
, it follows that
\colim\phi\sqsupseteq\sup\chi(\phi)
. The converse inequality follows from that

\displaystyle\colim\phi

\displaystyle=\colim\sup_{x\in I}X(-,x)=\sup I\sqsubseteq\sup\chi(\phi).\qed

Corollary 17.21
.

A functor
f\colon X\longrightarrow Y
between complete real-enriched categories is Yoneda continuous if and only if
f\colon X_{0}\longrightarrow Y_{0}
is Scott continuous.

As another consequence of Proposition
17.20
we present a useful characterization of ideals of a separated and complete real-enriched category.

Proposition 17.22
.

Suppose
X
is a separated and complete real-enriched category. Then, a weight
\phi
of
X
is an ideal if and only if

(i)

\phi(x)>0
for some
x\in X
.

(ii)

\phi(x\vee y)=\phi(x)\wedge\phi(y)
for all
x,y\in X
, where
x\vee y
denotes the join in
X_{0}
.

(iii)

\phi(r\otimes x)=1
whenever
r<\phi(x)
.

Proof.

We prove the necessity first. Since
\phi
is an ideal, it is inhabited, hence there is some
x
for which
\phi(x)>0
. In the following we use Proposition
17.20
to check that
\phi
satisfies both (ii) and (iii). Pick an ideal
I
of
X_{0}
such that
\phi=\Lambda(I)
. We index the elements of
I
by itself; that means, we write an element of
I
as
x_{i}
, with
i\leq j
if
x_{i}\sqsubseteq x_{j}
in
X_{0}
. Then

\displaystyle\phi(x\vee y)

\displaystyle=\sup_{i\in I}\inf_{j\geq i}X(x\vee y,x_{j})

\displaystyle=\sup_{i\in I}\inf_{j\geq i}X(x,x_{j})\wedge X(y,x_{j})

\displaystyle=\sup_{i\in I}\Big(\inf_{j\geq i}X(x,x_{j})\wedge\inf_{j\geq i}X(y,x_{j})\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big(\sup_{i\in I}\inf_{j\geq i}X(x,x_{j})\Big)\wedge\Big(\sup_{i\in I}\inf_{j\geq i}X(y,x_{j})\Big)

\displaystyle=\phi(x)\wedge\phi(y),

which shows that
\phi
satisfies (ii). For (iii), assume that
\phi(x)>r
. Then there exists
i_{0}\in I
such that
\inf_{j\geq i_{0}}X(x,x_{j})\geq r
, hence

\displaystyle\phi(r\otimes x)

\displaystyle=\sup_{i\in I}\inf_{j\geq i}X(r\otimes x,x_{j})

\displaystyle=\sup_{i\in I}\inf_{j\geq i}(r\rightarrow X(x,x_{j}))

\displaystyle\geq r\rightarrow\inf_{j\geq i_{0}}X(x,x_{j})

\displaystyle=1.

Now we prove the sufficiency. Let

I=\{x\in X\mid\phi(x)=1\}.

By (i) and (iii) one sees that
I
is not empty. By (ii) one sees that
I
is directed. So
I
is an ideal of
X_{0}
. It is clear that
\Lambda(I)\leq\phi
. To see that
\phi
is an idea, it suffices to check that
\phi(x)\leq\Lambda(I)(x)
for all
x\in X
. If
\phi(x)>r
, then
\phi(r\otimes x)=1
by (iii), hence
r\otimes x\in I
and consequently,
\Lambda(I)(x)\geq X(x,r\otimes x)\geq r
. Then
\phi(x)\leq\Lambda(I)(x)
by arbitrariness of
r
. ∎

Corollary 17.23
.

For each separated and complete real-enriched category
X
, the set
\mathcal{I}X
of ideals of
X
is closed in
[0,1]^{X}
(ordered pointwise) under meets and directed joins.

Proof.

Let
\{\phi_{i}\}_{i\in J}
be a subset of
\mathcal{I}X
. The meet
\inf_{i\in J}\phi_{i}
clearly satisfies (i) and (ii) in Proposition
17.22
. It remains to check it also satisfies the condition (iii). If
r<\inf_{i\in J}\phi_{i}(x)
, then for each
i\in J
,
r<\phi_{i}(x)
, hence
\phi_{i}(r\otimes x)=1
and consequently,
\inf_{i\in J}\phi_{i}(r\otimes x)=1
. Likewise,
\mathcal{I}X
is closed in
[0,1]^{X}
under directed joins. ∎

Theorem 17.24
.

(Lai and Zhang
[
51
]
)

For each separated and complete real-enriched category
X
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

X
is a real-enriched continuous lattice.

(2)

X_{0}
is a continuous lattice and for each
x\in X
and each ideal
\phi
of
X
,

X(x,\colim\phi)=\inf_{y\ll x}\phi(y),

where
\ll
denotes the way below relation in
X_{0}
.

(3)

X_{0}
is a continuous lattice and the map

{\sf d}\colon X\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}X,\quad{\sf d}(x)=\sup_{y\ll x}X(-,y)

is a functor, where
\ll
denotes the way below relation in
X_{0}
.

Proof.

(1)\Rightarrow(2)
Since
\mathcal{I}X
is closed in
[0,1]^{X}
under meets and directed joins, then
(\mathcal{I}X)_{0}
is a continuous lattice since so is
[0,1]
. The adjunction
\wayb\dashv\colim\colon\mathcal{I}X\longrightarrow X
ensures that
X_{0}
is a retract of
(\mathcal{I}X)_{0}
in the category of dcpos, so
X_{0}
is a continuous lattice.
To see that

X(x,\colim\phi)=\inf_{y\ll x}\phi(y)

for all
x\in X
and all
\phi\in\mathcal{I}X
, it suffices to show that the left adjoint
\wayb\colon A\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}A
of
\colim\colon\mathcal{I}A\longrightarrow A
is given by
\wayb x=\sup_{y\ll x}{\sf y}(y)
, because

\mathcal{I}X\Big(\sup_{y\ll x}{\sf y}(y),\phi\Big)=\sub_{X}\Big(\sup_{y\ll x}{\sf y}(y),\phi\Big)=\inf_{y\ll x}\phi(y).

Since
x
is a colimit of the ideal
\sup_{y\ll x}{\sf y}(y)
, then
\wayb x\leq\sup_{y\ll x}{\sf y}(y)
. Since
\sup\chi(\wayb x)=\colim\wayb x=x
by Proposition
17.20
, then every element way below
x
in
X_{0}
belongs to
\chi(\wayb x)
, so
\sup_{y\ll x}{\sf y}(y)\leq\wayb x
.

(2)\Rightarrow(3)
Obvious.

(3)\Rightarrow(1)

By virtue of Theorem
4.5
we only need to show that
{\sf d}\colon X_{0}\longrightarrow(\mathcal{I}X)_{0}
is left adjoint to
\colim\colon(\mathcal{I}X)_{0}\longrightarrow X_{0}
. This follows directly from Proposition
17.20
, which guarantees that for each
x\in X
,
\sup_{y\ll x}X(-,y)
is the smallest ideal of
X
(w.r.t. underlying order) that has
x
as a colimit.
∎

Proposition 17.25
.

Suppose
X
is a real-enriched continuous lattice. Then for all
x,y\in X
,

\mathfrak{w}(y,x)=\sup_{z\ll x}X(y,z)=\sup_{z\ll x}\mathfrak{w}(y,z),

where
\ll
refers to the way below relation in the complete lattice
X_{0}
.

Proof.

The first equality follows from the argument of Theorem
17.24
. The second equality follows from the first one and the interpolative property of the way below relation in
X_{0}
.
∎

Corollary 17.26
.

Suppose
X
is a separated and complete real-enriched category. If
X_{0}
is a continuous lattice and for all
p\in[0,1]
, the map

X_{0}\longrightarrow X_{0},\quad x\mapsto p\multimap x

is Scott continuous, then
X
is a real-enriched continuous lattice.

Proof.

We show that for each
x\in X
and each ideal
\phi
of
X
,

X(x,\colim\phi)=\inf_{y\ll x}\phi(y),

where
\ll
denotes the way below relation in
X_{0}
.

On the one hand, since
\{y\in X\mid y\ll x\}
is a directed set of
X_{0}
with join
x
, the conical weight
\sup_{y\ll x}X(-,y)
is an ideal of
X
with
x
as colimit, then

X(x,\colim\phi)\geq\mathcal{I}X\Big(\sup_{y\ll x}{\sf y}(y),\phi\Big)=\inf_{y\ll x}\phi(y).

On the other hand, for all
p\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle p\leq X(x,\colim\phi)\implies

\displaystyle x\leq p\multimap\colim\phi

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle x\leq\sup_{d\in\chi(\phi)}(p\multimap d)

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\forall y\ll x,~y\leq p\multimap d~\text{for some}~d\in\chi(\phi)

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\forall y\ll x,~p\leq X(y,d)~\text{for some}~d\in\chi(\phi),

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\forall y\ll x,p\leq\sup_{d\in\chi(\phi)}X(y,d)

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\forall{y\ll x},p\leq\phi(y)

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle p\leq\inf_{y\ll x}\phi(y).

Therefore,
X(x,\colim\phi)\leq\inf_{y\ll x}\phi(y)
.
∎

Example 17.27
.

For each continuous t-norm
\with
,
{\sf V}^{\rm op}=([0,1],\alpha_{R})
is a real-enriched continuous lattice. We use Corollary
17.26
to verify the claim. First, since the underlying order of
{\sf V}^{\rm op}
is the opposite of the usual order between real numbers,
({\sf V}^{\rm op})_{0}
is a continuous lattice. Second, since the cotensor
p\multimap x
of
p
with
x
in
{\sf V}^{\rm op}
is given by
p\with x
, the map
p\multimap-\colon({\sf V}^{\rm op})_{0}\longrightarrow({\sf V}^{\rm op})_{0}
is Scott continuous.

Theorem 17.28
.

(Lai and Zhang
[
51
]
)

The following are equivalent:

(1)

Every real-enriched completely distributive lattice is a real-enriched domain.

(2)

The real-enriched category
{\sf V}=([0,1],\alpha_{L})
is a real-enriched domain.

(3)

The implication operator
\rightarrow\colon[0,1]^{2}\longrightarrow[0,1]
is continuous at every point off the diagonal.

(4)

For each
\mathbb{P}^{\dagger}
-algebra
X
, the inclusion
\mathcal{I}X\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}X
is a right adjoint.

(5)

The copresheaf monad
\mathbb{P}^{\dagger}=(\mathcal{P}^{\dagger},{\sf m}^{\dagger},{\sf y}^{\dagger})
distributes over the ideal monad
\mathbb{I}=(\mathcal{I},{\sf m},{\sf y})
.

Proof.

(1)\Rightarrow(2)
Obvious.

(2)\Rightarrow(3)
By Theorem
17.24
, it suffices to show that if

{\sf d}\colon{\sf V}\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}{\sf V},\quad{\sf d}(x)(t)=\begin{cases}t\rightarrow 0&x=0,\\
\sup\limits_{y<x}(t\rightarrow y)&x>0\end{cases}

is a functor, then the implication operator
\rightarrow\colon[0,1]^{2}\longrightarrow[0,1]
is continuous at every point off the diagonal.

Suppose on the contrary that
\rightarrow\colon[0,1]^{2}\longrightarrow[0,1]
is not continuous at some point off the diagonal. Then there are idempotent elements
p,q>0
such that the restriction of
\&
on
[p,q]
is isomorphic to the Łukasiewicz t-norm. Pick
x\in(p,q)
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}{\sf V}({\sf d}(x),{\sf d}(p))

\displaystyle=\inf_{t\in[0,1]}\Big(\sup_{y<x}(t\rightarrow y)\rightarrow\sup_{z<p}(t\rightarrow z)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{y<x}(x\rightarrow y)\rightarrow\sup_{z<p}(x\rightarrow z)

\displaystyle=q\rightarrow p

\displaystyle<x\rightarrow p,

which shows that
{\sf d}\colon{\sf V}\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}{\sf V}
is not a functor, a contradiction.

(3)\Rightarrow(4)
It suffices to show that for each
\mathbb{P}^{\dagger}
-algebra
X
,
\mathcal{I}X
is closed in
\mathcal{P}X
under meets and cotensors. That
\mathcal{I}X
is closed in
\mathcal{P}X
under meets is ensured by Lemma
17.23
, so we only need to show that
\mathcal{I}X
is closed in
\mathcal{P}X
under cotensors.

For
p\in[0,1]
and
\phi\in\mathcal{I}X
, set

D=\{d\in X\mid p\leq\phi(d)\}.

Then
D
is a directed subset of
X_{0}
. First we show that

\{p\multimap y\mid y\in\chi(\phi)\}\subseteq D.

Since
\phi=\sup_{z\in\chi(\phi)}X(-,z)
, then for all
y\in\chi(\phi)
,

\displaystyle p\rightarrow\phi(p\multimap y)

\displaystyle=p\rightarrow\sup_{z\in\chi(\phi)}X(p\multimap y,z)

\displaystyle\geq p\rightarrow X(p\multimap y,y)

\displaystyle=X(p\multimap y,p\multimap y)

\displaystyle=1,

which implies that
p\multimap y\in D
.

Next, let
\rho=\sup_{d\in D}X(-,d).
We wish to show that
p\rightarrow\phi=\rho
, from which the conclusion follows since
\rho
is an ideal of
X
and
p\rightarrow\phi
is the cotensor of
p
with
\phi
in
\mathcal{P}X
.
That
\rho\leq p\rightarrow\phi
is clear. It remains to check that
p\rightarrow\phi(x)\leq\rho(x)
for all
x\in X
. If
p\leq\phi(x)
, then
x\in D
and

\rho(x)=\sup_{d\in D}X(x,d)\geq X(x,x)=1=p\rightarrow\phi(x).

If
p>\phi(x)
, then the implication operator
\rightarrow
is continuous at
(p,\phi(x))
, so,

\displaystyle p\rightarrow\phi(x)

\displaystyle=p\rightarrow\sup_{y\in\chi(\phi)}X(x,y)

\displaystyle=\sup_{y\in\chi(\phi)}(p\rightarrow X(x,y))

\displaystyle=\sup_{y\in\chi(\phi)}X(x,p\multimap y)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{d\in D}X(x,d)

\displaystyle(p\multimap y\in D)

\displaystyle=\rho(x).

(4)\Rightarrow(5)
Proposition
15.9
.

(5)\Rightarrow(1)
Theorem
16.4
.
∎

Remark 17.29
.

(i)

Continuity of the implication operator off the diagonal is related to the equivalence of the logic formulas

\exists x(p\rightarrow q(x))\quad\text{and}\quad p\rightarrow\exists xq(x).

So, in many-valued logic whether complete distributivity implies continuity depends on the structure of the truth-values.

(ii)

Ideals of real-enriched categories may be thought of as analogue of ind-objects of (set-valued) categories, but in contrast to the fact that the category of ind-objects of a small category with finite colimits is complete and cocomplete (see e.g. Johnstone
[
37
, Chapter VI, Section 1]
), the real-enriched category
\mathcal{I}X
of ideals of a complete real-enriched category
X
may fail to be complete. Actually, by Proposition
15.8
and the above theorem, in order that
\mathcal{I}X
be complete for every complete real-enriched category
X
, it is necessary and sufficient that the implication operator of the continuous t-norm
\with
is continuous at every point off the diagonal.

Let

[0,1]\text{-}{\bf ConLat}

be the category of real-enriched continuous lattices and Yoneda continuous functors preserving enriched limits.
When the implication operator of the t-norm
\with
is continuous at each point off the diagonal,
[0,1]\text{-}{\bf ConLat}
is the Eilenberg-Moore category of the composite monad
\mathbb{I}\circ\mathbb{P}^{\dagger}
of the copresheaf monad and the ideal monad, hence monadic over
[0,1]\text{-}{\bf Cat}
. In this case,
[0,1]\text{-}{\bf ConLat}
is also monadic over the category of sets, as we see below.

Theorem 17.30
.

If the implication operator of
\with
is continuous at every point off the diagonal, then the forgetful functor
U\colon[0,1]\text{-}{\bf ConLat}\longrightarrow{\bf Set}
is monadic.

We say that a functor
k\colon X\longrightarrow X
is a
kernel operator
if
k^{2}=k
and
k(x)\sqsubseteq x
in
X_{0}
for all
x\in X
. If
k\colon X\longrightarrow X
is a kernel operator, then
k\colon X\longrightarrow k(X)
is right adjoint to the inclusion
k(X)\longrightarrow X
, so
k(X)
is a retract of
X
in
[0,1]\text{-}{\bf Cat}
. In particular, if
X
is a real-enriched continuous lattice and
k\colon X\longrightarrow X
is a Yoneda continuous kernel operator, then
k\colon X\longrightarrow k(X)
is a Yoneda continuous right adjoint, hence
k(X)
is a real-enriched continuous lattice by Corollary
15.7
.
The verification of the following lemma is routine.

Lemma 17.31
.

Suppose that
X
is a separated and complete real-enriched category. If
R
is an equivalence relation on
X
subject to the following conditions:

(i)

R
is closed w.r.t. directed joins in
X_{0}\times X_{0}
,

(ii)

R
is closed w.r.t. meets in
X_{0}\times X_{0}
,

(iii)

If
(x,y)\in R
, then
(r\multimap x,r\multimap y)\in R
for all
r\in[0,1]
,

then the map
k\colon X\longrightarrow X
that sends each
x\in X
to the meet of
\{y\mid(x,y)\in R\}
in
X_{0}
is a Yoneda continuous kernel operator.

Proof of Theorem
17.30
.

Since the forgetful functor
U\colon[0,1]\text{-}{\bf ConLat}\longrightarrow{\bf Set}
is the composite of the forgetful functors
[0,1]\text{-}{\bf ConLat}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{\dagger}\text{-}{\bf Alg}
and
\mathbb{P}^{\dagger}\text{-}{\bf Alg}\longrightarrow{\bf Set}
, it is a right adjoint. So it suffices to check that it creates split coequalizers.

Let
f,g\colon X\longrightarrow Y
be a parallel pair of morphisms in
[0,1]\text{-}{\bf ConLat}
and let
h\colon Y\longrightarrow Z
be a split coequalizer of
f,g
in
{\bf Set}
. By definition there exist morphisms
Z\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.48047pt\raise 5.31596pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.31596pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.89511pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.79022pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>Y\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.48808pt\raise 5.99652pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.63542pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{j}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.34486pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 18.68973pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>X
in
{\bf Set}
such that

h\circ f=h\circ g,~f\circ j={\rm id},~h\circ i={\rm id},~g\circ j=i\circ h.

Let

R=\{(y_{1},y_{2})\in Y\times Y\mid h(y_{1})=h(y_{2})\}.

It is not hard to check that
(y_{1},y_{2})\in R
if and only if there exists
(x_{1},x_{2})\in X\times X
such that
g(x_{1})=g(x_{2})
,
y_{1}=f(x_{1})
,
y_{2}=f(x_{2})
. With help of this fact, one readily verifies that
R
satisfies the conditions (i)-(iii) in Lemma
17.31
, hence determines a Yoneda continuous kernel operator
k\colon Y\longrightarrow Y
. Since
k(Y)
is a real-enriched continuous lattice with an underlying set equipotent to
Z
,
Z
can be made into a real-enriched continuous lattice so that
h\colon Y\longrightarrow Z
is a Yoneda continuous right adjoint. This proves that the forgetful functor
[0,1]\text{-}{\bf ConLat}\longrightarrow{\bf Set}
creates split coequalizers. ∎

Suppose the implication operator of
\with
is continuous at each point off the diagonal and
F\colon{\bf Set}\longrightarrow[0,1]\text{-}{\bf ConLat}
is the left adjoint of the forgetful functor
U\colon[0,1]\text{-}{\bf ConLat}\longrightarrow{\bf Set}
. In the following we show that the monad in the category of sets induced by the adjunction
F\dashv U
is a submonad of the double contravariant fuzzy powerset monad
(\mathscr{E},{\sf m},{\sf e})
in Section
13
.

Let
X
be a real-enriched category. Following Antoniuk and Waszkiewicz
[
5
]
we define a
filter
of
X
to be an ideal of its opposite
X^{\rm op}
. In other words, a filter of
X
is a coweight
\psi
of
X
such that

\psi=\sup_{i\in D}\inf_{j\geq i}X(x_{j},-)

for some net
\{x_{i}\}_{i\in D}
that is
backward Cauchy
in the sense that

\sup_{i\in D}\inf_{k\geq j\geq i}X(x_{k},x_{j})=1.

Definition 17.32
.

Let
X
be a set. Then a filter of the real-enriched category
([0,1]^{X},\sub_{X})
is called a conical filter of
X
.

Proposition 17.33
.

(Morsi
[
63
]
)
Let
X
be a set. Then,
\mathfrak{F}\colon[0,1]^{X}\longrightarrow[0,1]
is a conical filter if and only if for all
\lambda,\mu\in[0,1]^{X}
and all
r\in[0,1]
,

(CF1)

\sub_{X}(\lambda,\mu)\leq\mathfrak{F}(\lambda)\rightarrow\mathfrak{F}(\mu)
;

(CF2)

\mathfrak{F}(1_{X})=1
;

(CF3)

\mathfrak{F}(\lambda)\wedge\mathfrak{F}(\mu)=\mathfrak{F}(\lambda\wedge\mu)
;

(CF4)

\mathfrak{F}(r\rightarrow\lambda)=1
whenever
\mathfrak{F}(\lambda)>r
.

Proof.

Follows from Corollary
17.22
directly. ∎

Proposition 17.34
.

Let
X
be a set.

(i)

For each family
\{\mathfrak{F}_{i}\}_{i\in J}
of conical filters of
X
, the meet
\inf_{i\in J}\mathfrak{F}_{i}
is a conical filter of
X
.

(ii)

For each directed family
\{\mathfrak{F}_{i}\}_{i\in J}
of conical filters of
X
, the join
\sup_{i\in J}\mathfrak{F}_{i}
is a conical filter of
X
.

(iii)

If the implication operator
\rightarrow\colon[0,1]\times[0,1]\longrightarrow[0,1]
is continuous at each point off the diagonal, then for each
p\in[0,1]
and each conical filter
\mathfrak{F}
of
X
,
p\rightarrow\mathfrak{F}
is a conical filter of
X
.

Proof.

(i) and (ii) are a special case of Corollary
17.23
; (iii) is a special case of the implication
(3)\Rightarrow(4)
in Theorem
17.28
. ∎

For each set
X
let
\mathscr{F}X
be the set of all conical filters of
X
; or equivalently,
\mathscr{F}X=\mathcal{I}\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}X
with
X
viewed as a discrete real-enriched category. For each function
f\colon X\longrightarrow Y
and each conical filter
\mathfrak{F}
of
X
, the function

f(\mathfrak{F})\colon[0,1]^{Y}\longrightarrow[0,1],\quad\mu\mapsto\mathfrak{F}(\mu\circ f)

is a conical filter of
Y
. In this way we obtain a functor
\mathscr{F}\colon{\bf Set}\longrightarrow{\bf Set},
called the
conical filter functor
. The conical filter functor
\mathscr{F}
is a subfunctor of the double contravariant fuzzy powerset functor
\mathscr{E}\colon{\bf Set}\longrightarrow{\bf Set}
, also a subfunctor of the functor
\mathscr{C}\colon{\bf Set}\longrightarrow{\bf Set}
in Section
16
.

Lemma 17.35
.

(Lai, D & G Zhang
[
53
]
)

The following are equivalent:

(1)

The implication operator
\rightarrow\colon[0,1]\times[0,1]\longrightarrow[0,1]
is continuous at each point off the diagonal
\{(x,x)\mid x\in[0,1]\}
.

(2)

The conical filter functor
\mathscr{F}\colon{\bf Set}\longrightarrow{\bf Set}
is a submonad of the double contravariant fuzzy powerset monad
(\mathscr{E},{\sf m},{\sf e})
.

Proof.

(1)\Rightarrow(2)
By Lemma
16.7
we only need to check that for each set
X
and each conical filter
\mathcal{F}
of
\mathscr{F}X
, the function

k(\mathcal{F})\colon[0,1]^{X}\longrightarrow[0,1],\quad\lambda\mapsto\mathcal{F}(\widehat{\lambda})

is a conical filter of
X
, where the function
\widehat{\lambda}\colon\mathscr{F}X\longrightarrow[0,1]
maps each conical filter
\mathfrak{F}
of
X
to
\mathfrak{F}(\lambda)
.

Since
\mathcal{F}
is a conical filter of
\mathscr{F}X
, there is a directed subset
D
of
[0,1]^{\mathscr{F}X}
(ordered pointwise) such that

\mathcal{F}=\sup_{\xi\in D}\sub_{\mathscr{F}X}(\xi,-).

Then for each
\lambda\in[0,1]^{X}
it holds that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\widehat{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\sup_{\xi\in D}\sub_{\mathscr{F}X}(\xi,\widehat{\lambda})=\sup_{\xi\in D}\inf_{\mathfrak{F}\in\mathscr{F}X}(\xi(\mathfrak{F})\rightarrow\mathfrak{F})(\lambda),

which shows that

k(\mathcal{F})=\sup_{\xi\in D}\inf_{\mathfrak{F}\in\mathscr{F}X}(\xi(\mathfrak{F})\rightarrow\mathfrak{F}),

hence
k(\mathcal{F})
is a conical filter of
X
by Proposition
17.34
. The conical filter
k(\mathcal{F})
is called the
diagonal filter
or the
Kowalsky sum
of
\mathcal{F}
.

(2)\Rightarrow(1)
First we show that if the conical filter functor
\mathscr{F}\colon{\bf Set}\longrightarrow{\bf Set}
is a submonad of
(\mathscr{E},{\sf m},{\sf e})
, then for each set
X
, each conical filter
\mathfrak{F}
of
X
and each
r\in[0,1]
, the function
r\rightarrow\mathfrak{F}
is a conical filter of
X
. Let
\mathfrak{i}\colon\mathscr{F}\longrightarrow\mathscr{E}
be the inclusion transformation and
\mathcal{F}
be the conical filter of
\mathscr{F}X
generated by the subset

\{\xi\colon\mathscr{F}X\longrightarrow[0,1]\mid\xi(\mathfrak{F})\geq r\}

of
[0,1]^{\mathscr{F}X}
. Since
\mathscr{F}
is a submonad of
(\mathscr{E},{\sf m},{\sf e})
, then
{\sf m}_{X}\circ(\mathfrak{i}*\mathfrak{i})_{X}(\mathcal{F})
is a conical filter of
X
. Since for each
\lambda\in[0,1]^{X}
,

{\sf m}_{X}\circ(\mathfrak{i}*\mathfrak{i})_{X}(\mathcal{F})(\lambda)=\mathcal{F}(\widehat{\lambda})=\sup_{\xi(\mathfrak{F})\geq r}\sub_{\mathscr{F}X}(\xi,\widehat{\lambda})=r\rightarrow\mathfrak{F}(\lambda),

it follows that
r\rightarrow\mathfrak{F}
is a conical filter of
X
, as desired.

Now we prove the conclusion.
Suppose on the contrary that the implication operator
\rightarrow\colon[0,1]\times[0,1]\longrightarrow[0,1]
is not continuous at some point not on the diagonal. Then there exist idempotent elements
p,q
of
\&
such that
0<p<q
and that the restriction of
\&
on
[p,q]
is isomorphic to the Łukasiewicz t-norm. Pick
t,s\in(p,q)
such that
p<s<t\rightarrow p<q
.

Let
X=[0,1]
and pick a strictly increasing sequence
\{b_{n}\}_{n}
of
[0,1]
that converges to
1
. Consider the conical filter
\mathfrak{F}
of
X
generated by the subset

\{1_{B_{n}}\mid n\geq 1\}

of
[0,1]^{X}
, where
B_{n}=\{b_{m}\mid m\geq n\}
; that means for all
\mu\in[0,1]^{X}
,

\mathfrak{F}(\mu)=\sup_{n\geq 1}\inf_{m\geq n}\mu(b_{m}).

In the following we derive a contradiction by showing that
t\rightarrow\mathfrak{F}
is not a conical filter of
X
. Consider the function

\lambda\colon X\longrightarrow[0,1],\quad\lambda(x)=px.

Since

\mathfrak{F}(\lambda)=p\sup_{n\geq 1}\inf_{m\geq n}b_{m}=p,

it follows that

s<t\rightarrow p=(t\rightarrow\mathfrak{F})(\lambda).

But,

\displaystyle(t\rightarrow\mathfrak{F})(s\rightarrow\lambda)

\displaystyle=t\rightarrow\sup_{n\geq 1}\inf_{m\geq n}(s\rightarrow\lambda(b_{m}))

\displaystyle=t\rightarrow\sup_{n\geq 1}\inf_{m\geq n}\lambda(b_{m})

\displaystyle(\lambda(b_{m})<p<s))

\displaystyle=t\rightarrow p

\displaystyle<1.

This shows, by Proposition
17.33
, that
t\rightarrow\mathfrak{F}
is not a conical filter of
X
. ∎

Suppose the implication operator
\rightarrow\colon[0,1]\times[0,1]\longrightarrow[0,1]
is continuous at each point off the diagonal and
F\colon{\bf Set}\longrightarrow[0,1]\text{-}{\bf ConLat}
is the left adjoint of the forgetful functor
U\colon[0,1]\text{-}{\bf ConLat}\longrightarrow{\bf Set}
. Then, it is readily verified that the monad induced by
F\dashv U
is the conical filter momad
(\mathscr{F},{\sf m},{\sf e})
. This proves:

Proposition 17.36
.

The conical filter functor
\mathscr{F}\colon{\bf Set}\longrightarrow{\bf Set}
is a submonad of the double contravariant fuzzy powerset monad
(\mathscr{E},{\sf m},{\sf e})
if and only if the implication operator of the continuous t-norm is continuous at each point off the diagonal. In this case, the Eilenberg-Moore category of
(\mathscr{F},{\sf m},{\sf e})
is the category of real-enriched continuous lattices and Yoneda continuous functors preserving enriched limits.

Question 17.37
.

A conical filter
\mathfrak{F}
of a set
X
is
proper
if
\mathfrak{F}(p_{X})=p
for all
p\in[0,1]
. Assigning to each set
X
the set of all conical proper filters of
X
gives rise to a subfunctor of conical filter functor
\mathscr{F}
.
It is not hard to check that if the implication operator
\rightarrow\colon[0,1]\times[0,1]\longrightarrow[0,1]
is continuous at each point off the diagonal, then the conical proper filter functor is a submonad of
(\mathscr{F},{\sf m},{\sf e})
. What are the Eilenberg-Moore algebras of this monad?

Question 17.38
.

Suppose
\mathbb{T}
is a submonad of the presheaf monad over which the copresheaf monad
\mathbb{P}^{\dagger}
distributes. By Corollary
15.12
, the category
\mathbb{T}\text{-}{\bf CCAlg}
of complete and continuous
\mathbb{T}
-algebras and right adjoint
\mathbb{T}
-homomorphisms is monadic over
[0,1]\text{-}{\bf Cat}
. Is
\mathbb{T}\text{-}{\bf CCAlg}
monadic over the category of sets too?

Question 17.39
.

When does the copresheaf monad
\mathbb{P}^{\dagger}
distributes over the the flat weight monad or over the conically flat weight monad?

18.
Saturation

For each class of weights
\mathcal{T}
, no matter saturated or not, we write

\mathcal{T}\text{-}{\sf Alg}

for the category of separated
\mathcal{T}
-cocomplete real-enriched categories and
\mathcal{T}
-colimits preserving functors. If
\mathcal{T}
happens to be saturated, then
{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}
is the category of Eilenberg-Moore algebras of the monad
\mathbb{T}=(\mathcal{T},{\sf m},{\sf y})
. In this section, we show that for each class of weights
\mathcal{T}
, there is a unique saturated class of weights
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
such that
{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}=\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}
, which implies, in particular, that
{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}
is category of Eilenberg-Moore algebras of a unique submonad of the presheaf monad.

The main result of section, namely Theorem
18.1
, is a special case of a result of Albert and Kelly
[
1
]
on saturation of class of weights in enriched category theory.

Theorem 18.1
.

For each class of weights
\mathcal{T}
, there exists a unique saturated class of weights
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
, called the saturation of
\mathcal{T}
, such that

(i)

a real-enriched category
X
is
\mathcal{T}
-cocomplete if and only if it is
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
-cocomplete;

(ii)

a functor
f\colon X\longrightarrow Y
between
\mathcal{T}
-cocomplete real-enriched categories preserves
\mathcal{T}
-colimits if and only if it preserves
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
-colimits.

In particular,
{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}=\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}
.

To prove the theorem, we need to introduce two classes of weights:
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}
and
\overline{\mathcal{T}}
.

For each real-enriched category
X
, define a subcategory

\widehat{\mathcal{T}}X

of
\mathcal{P}X
by putting a weight
\phi
of
X
in
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}X
if, for any morphism
g\colon Y\longrightarrow Z
of
{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}
and any functor
f\colon X\longrightarrow Y
, the colimit of
f
weighted by
\phi
always exists and is preserved by
g
, i.e.
g(\colim_{\phi}f)=\colim_{\phi}(g\circ f).

It is readily verified that

(i)

\widehat{\mathcal{T}}
is a class of weights and contains the class
\mathcal{T}
;

(ii)

\widehat{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}}=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}
;

(iii)

\widehat{\mathcal{T}}
is the largest class of weights for which
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}={\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}.

Now we define the the class
\overline{\mathcal{T}}
.
Suppose
X
is a real-enriched category,
A
is a subset of
\mathcal{P}X
, viewed as a subcategory. We say that
A
is
closed under
\mathcal{T}
-colimits
if, for each
\phi\in\mathcal{T}A
, the colimit of the inclusion functor
i\colon A\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}X
weighted by
\phi
belongs to
A
.
It is readily seen that if
A\subseteq\mathcal{P}X
is closed under
\mathcal{T}
-colimits, then for each functor
g\colon K\longrightarrow A
and each
\phi\in\mathcal{T}K
, the colimit of
i\circ g
weighted by
\phi
belongs to
A
.

For each real-enriched category
X
, let

\overline{\mathcal{T}}X

be the intersection of all subsets of
\mathcal{P}X
that contain all representable weights of
X
and are closed under
\mathcal{T}
-colimits. Then
\overline{\mathcal{T}}X
is the least such subset of
\mathcal{P}X
. Let

•

\mathfrak{i}_{X}\colon\overline{\mathcal{T}}X\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}X
be the inclusion functor; and

•

{\sf t}_{X}\colon X\longrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{T}}X
be the Yoneda embedding with codomain restricted to
\overline{\mathcal{T}}X
.

The composite
\mathfrak{i}_{X}\circ{\sf t}_{X}
is the Yoneda embedding
{\sf y}_{X}\colon X\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}X
.

Lemma 18.2
.

For each real-enriched category
X
,
\mathcal{T}X\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{T}}X
.

Proof.

Because for each weight
\phi
of
X
,
\phi
is the colimit of
\mathfrak{i}_{X}\circ{\sf t}_{X}
weighted by
\phi
. ∎

Lemma 18.3
.

For each functor
f\colon X\longrightarrow Y
and each
\phi\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}X
,
f_{\exists}(\phi)\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}Y
.

Proof.

Let

Z=\{\phi\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}X\mid f_{\exists}(\phi)\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}Y\}.

It is clear that
Z
contains all representable weights of
X
. If we can show that
Z
is closed under
\mathcal{T}
-colimits, then
Z=\overline{\mathcal{T}}X
and the conclusion follows.

Let
j\colon Z\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}X
be the inclusion functor. We show that for each

\Phi\in\mathcal{T}Z
, the colimit of
j
weighted by
\Phi
belongs to
Z
.

Since
f_{\exists}\colon\mathcal{P}X\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}Y
preserves colimits, then
f_{\exists}({\colim}_{\Phi}j)={\colim}_{\Phi}(f_{\exists}\circ j)
. Since
f_{\exists}\circ j
factors through
\overline{\mathcal{T}}Y
by definition of
Z
,
\overline{\mathcal{T}}Y
is closed under
\mathcal{T}
-colimits, it follows that

{\colim}_{\Phi}(f_{\exists}\circ j)\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}Y,

which implies that
{\colim}_{\Phi}j\in Z
, as desired.
∎

The above two lemmas imply that
\overline{\mathcal{T}}
is a class of weights on
[0,1]\text{-}{\bf Cat}
. The assignment
X\mapsto\overline{\mathcal{T}}X
defines a functor

\overline{\mathcal{T}}\colon[0,1]\text{-}{\bf Cat}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg},

which is indeed left adjoint to the forgetful functor
U\colon{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}\longrightarrow[0,1]\text{-}{\bf Cat}
, as we see below. This is a special case of a general result of Kelly
[
39
]
in the theory of enriched categories.

Proposition 18.4
.

The functor
\overline{\mathcal{T}}\colon[0,1]\text{-}{\bf Cat}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}
is left adjoint to the forgetful functor
U\colon{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}\longrightarrow[0,1]\text{-}{\bf Cat}
.

To prove Proposition
18.4
we need a lemma.

Lemma 18.5
.

For each
\mathcal{T}
-cocomplete real-enriched category
Y
,

\overline{\mathcal{T}}Y\subseteq\{\phi\in\mathcal{P}Y\mid\phi~\text{has a colimit}\}.

Proof.

It suffices to show that

W\coloneqq\{\phi\in\mathcal{P}Y\mid\phi~\text{has a colimit}\}

is closed under
\mathcal{T}
-colimits.

Let
j\colon W\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}Y
be the inclusion map. We wish to show that for each
\Phi\in\mathcal{T}W
, the colimit
\colim_{\Phi}j
, as a weight of
Y
, has a colimit. First of all, by Corollary
7.11
we have

{\colim}_{\Phi}j=\sup_{\phi\in W}\Phi(\phi)\with\phi.

Since every
\phi\in W
has a colimit, assigning to each
\phi\in W
its colimit defines a functor
h\colon W\longrightarrow Y
. Since
Y
is
\mathcal{T}
-cocomplete, the colimit of
h\colon W\longrightarrow Y
weighted by
\Phi
exists, say
b
. We claim that
b
is a colimit of the weight
\colim_{\Phi}j
of
Y
.
In fact, for all
y\in Y
,

\displaystyle Y(b,y)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}Y(\Phi\circ h^{*},{\sf y}(y))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}W(\Phi,h^{-1}({\sf y}(y)))

\displaystyle=\inf_{\phi\in W}(\Phi(\phi)\rightarrow Y(h(\phi),y))

\displaystyle=\inf_{\phi\in W}\Big[\Phi(\phi)\rightarrow\Big(\inf_{z\in Y}(\phi(z)\rightarrow Y(z,y))\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle=\inf_{z\in Y}\Big[\Big(\sup_{\phi\in W}\Phi(\phi)\with\phi(z)\Big)\rightarrow Y(z,y)\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}Y({\colim}_{\Phi}\thinspace j,{\sf y}(y)),

which shows that
b
is a colimit of the weight
\colim_{\Phi}j
of
Y
. ∎

Proof of Proposition
18.4
.

It suffices to show that for each functor
f\colon X\longrightarrow Y
with
Y
in
{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}
, there is a unique
\mathcal{T}
-colimits preserving functor
\overline{f}\colon\overline{\mathcal{T}}X\longrightarrow Y
such that
f=\overline{f}\circ{\sf t}_{X}
.

Since the real-enriched category
W
given in Lemma
18.5
is
\mathcal{T}
-cocomplete, it is readily verified that
\overline{\mathcal{T}}X
is contained in

\{\phi\in\mathcal{P}X\mid f_{\exists}(\phi)\in W\}.

Let
h\colon W\longrightarrow Y
be the functor that sends each
\phi
of
W
to its colimit. Then

\overline{f}\coloneqq h\circ f_{\exists}\colon\overline{\mathcal{T}}X\longrightarrow W\longrightarrow Y

satisfies the requirement. This proves the existence. To see the uniqueness, suppose

g\colon\overline{\mathcal{T}}X\longrightarrow Y
satisfies the requirement. Then

\{\phi\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}X\mid g(\phi)=\overline{f}(\phi)\}

contains all representable weights of
X
and is closed under
\mathcal{T}
-colimits. Hence,
g=\overline{f}
by definition of
\overline{\mathcal{T}}X
.
∎

For each
Y
of
{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}
, the functor
\colim\colon\overline{\mathcal{T}}Y\longrightarrow Y
sending each
\phi
to its colimit is the component of the counit of the adjunction
\overline{\mathcal{T}}\dashv U
in Proposition
18.4
. Hence for each morphism
g\colon Y\longrightarrow Z
of
{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}
, the following square is commutative:

\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{Z}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{T}}Y}
\textstyle{Y}
\scriptstyle{\colim}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{T}}Y}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{T}}Z}
\scriptstyle{g_{\exists}}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathcal{T}}Z}
\textstyle{Z}
\scriptstyle{\colim}

This fact will be used in the proof of Theorem
18.1
.

Proof of Theorem
18.1
.

For existence we show that
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}\coloneqq\widehat{\mathcal{T}}
satisfies the requirement. Since
{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}
, it remains to show that
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}
is saturated.

Since
\overline{\mathcal{T}}X
is closed under
\mathcal{T}
-colimits in
\mathcal{P}X
, if we can prove that
\overline{\mathcal{T}}X=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}X
for each real-enriched category
X
, then
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}X
is closed under
\mathcal{T}
-colimits, hence under
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}
-colimits, and consequently,
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}
is saturated.

First we prove that
\overline{\mathcal{T}}X\subseteq\widehat{\mathcal{T}}X
. That means, if
\phi\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}X
and
g\colon Y\longrightarrow Z
is a morphism in
{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}
, then for any functor
f\colon X\longrightarrow Y
, the colimit of
f
weighted by
\phi
exists and is preserved by
g
. Since
f_{\exists}(\phi)\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}Y
, the colimit of
f
weighted by
\phi
exists. By commutativity of the above square we have
\colim(g_{\exists}\circ f_{\exists}(\phi))=g(\colim f_{\exists}(\phi))
, hence the colimit of
f
weighted by
\phi
is preserved by
g
.

Next we prove that
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}X\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{T}}X
.
Since
{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}
, each
A
of
{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}
is
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}
-cocomplete.
Since
\overline{\mathcal{T}}X
belongs to
{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}
, it is
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}
-cocomplete. Since the inclusion
\mathfrak{i}_{X}\colon\overline{\mathcal{T}}X\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}X
preserves
\mathcal{T}
-colimits, it preserves
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}
-colimits too. Then, for each
\phi\in\widehat{\mathcal{T}}X
,

{\colim}_{\phi}\thinspace{\sf t}_{X}={\colim}_{\phi}(\mathfrak{i}_{X}\circ{\sf t}_{X})={\colim}_{\phi}\thinspace{\sf y}_{X}=\phi,

hence
\phi\in\overline{\mathcal{T}}X
.

To see uniqueness, suppose
\mathcal{S}
is a saturated class of weights with
\mathcal{S}\text{-}{\sf Alg}={\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}
. Then we prove that
\mathcal{S}=\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
by showing that
\mathcal{S}
is a subclass of
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}
and contains the class
\overline{\mathcal{T}}
.

Since
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}
is the largest class of weights for which
{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}\text{-}{\sf Alg}
,
\mathcal{S}
is a subclass of
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}
.
To see that
\mathcal{S}
contains the class
\overline{\mathcal{T}}
, first we show that
\mathcal{S}
contains the class
\mathcal{T}
; that is,
\mathcal{T}X\subseteq\mathcal{S}X
for each real-enriched category
X
. Since
\mathcal{S}
is saturated, then

•

\mathcal{S}X
is
\mathcal{S}
-cocomplete, hence
\mathcal{T}
-cocomplete;

•

the inclusion functor
j_{X}\colon\mathcal{S}X\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}X
preserves
\mathcal{S}
-colimits, hence
\mathcal{T}
-colimits.

Let
{\sf s}_{X}\colon X\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}X
be the Yoneda embedding with codomain restricted to
\mathcal{S}X
. Then for each
\phi\in\mathcal{T}X
we have

{\colim}_{\phi}\thinspace{\sf s}_{X}={\colim}_{\phi}(j_{X}\circ{\sf s}_{X})={\colim}_{\phi}\thinspace{\sf y}_{X}=\phi,

so
\phi\in\mathcal{S}X
and
\mathcal{T}X\subseteq\mathcal{S}X
.

Now we show that
\mathcal{S}
contains
\overline{\mathcal{T}}
. Since
\mathcal{S}
is saturated and contains the class
\mathcal{T}
, it follows that for each real-enriched category
X
,
\mathcal{S}X
is closed in
\mathcal{P}X
under
\mathcal{T}
-colimits, then
\overline{\mathcal{T}}X\subseteq\mathcal{S}X
.
∎
